Gestures: tap moves the pointer there and left-clicks; long press right-clicks; long press then drag holds button 1 while moving; pinch zooms about the pinch centre; two-finger drag pans the zoomed view, and at fit, where nothing pans, sends wheel events, one button 4/5 press-release per 40 px. `KEYS` in the breadcrumb opens the soft keyboard: each typed character goes as an X keysym press and release (Latin-1 codepoints direct, others `0x01000000 + codepoint`), and a strip above the keyboard carries `CTRL`, `ALT`, `SUPER`, `ESC`, `TAB`, `←`, `↑`, `↓`, `→`, `ENTER`, `DONE`, the three modifiers first because they arm the key that follows and stay sticky for exactly one. Leaving the route closes the channel and the ssh connection.
- Protocol: RFB 3.8, accepting 3.3 and 3.7 servers by their own rules; security `None` only. `ClientInit` shared. `SetPixelFormat` 32 bpp, depth 24, little-endian, true colour, red shift 16 / green 8 / blue 0, which is Flutter's `bgra8888`, so rects land in the framebuffer without a swizzle. `SetEncodings` in preference order ZRLE (16), CopyRect (1), Raw (0), DesktopSize (-223). One non-incremental `FramebufferUpdateRequest` first, incremental after, the next sent as soon as the previous update is painted, at most 24 per second (wayvnc's cap on nyx). ZRLE: one zlib stream for the whole session (`ZLibDecoder().startChunkedConversion`), 64 px tiles, all subencodings (raw, solid, packed palette 2/4/16 colours, plain RLE, palette RLE), CPIXEL at 3 bytes as the spec reads it for this format. Bell, cut text and colour-map messages are read and dropped. Pub's `dart_rfb` is not used: Raw and CopyRect only, and it owns its socket.
- Rendering: one `Uint8List` framebuffer sized by `ServerInit` or `DesktopSize`, rects blitted in, and one image per update, never one per rect. The builder measures paint time per 1080p update on the emulator and writes it in the evidence README. Failures are written to the debug log as `dazzle.vnc <host>: …`, 39.29's rule.
- Regression tests: the handshake against a scripted byte stream (version, security types, `ServerInit`) yields width, height and name and emits the exact client bytes; a security list without `None` maps to `auth unsupported`; a refused channel maps to `tunnel refused`; ZRLE vectors for every subencoding and a CopyRect land the expected pixels in the framebuffer; the pointer mapping from touch to framebuffer coordinates under fit and under zoom; key events for a letter, `ENTER`, `ESC` and a sticky `CTRL`; `SCREEN >` absent without a `vnc` host and present with one; disposing the route closes channel and client; `vnc` persists in `host_integrations` like `herdr`.
- Not built, waiting for the user's word: VNC password auth, a display port field, clipboard, cursor pseudo-encoding, more than one output.
- Version bumps with the build that ships it.

### 39.33 The pirate mascot: pixel sprites, splash animation, in-app states (user, 2026-09-03: "Recuerdas al búho? ya no será eso ahora será ese pirata, ojo, deben de ser sprites o animaciones… eso que te digo junto con las imágenes son el resultado a lograr")
- Overrides 39.8.1: the launcher mark is the pirate skeleton of `design/references/concept/dazzle-mascot-v0.png`, the raven goes. 3 and 10's stroke rules do not apply to the mascot: it is pixel art and nothing else, no anti-aliasing anywhere in its path.
- Grid: one 48×48 cell per frame, the figure about 26 wide by 38 tall with its feet on row 44, three values only: transparent, text primary `#E8E8E3`, accent `#B7FF2A` (the app's tokens, not the reference's hexes). Source of truth is text grids in `design/sprites/mascot/<state>/<nn>.txt`, 48 lines of 48 characters, `.` transparent, `#` primary, `a` accent. `design/tools/sprites.py` (python3 + Pillow through nix) assembles `assets/sprites/mascot-<state>.png`, frames left to right at 1×, and writes `assets/sprites/mascot.json` as `{"cell": 48, "states": {"<state>": {"frames": N, "fps": F, "loop": bool}}}`. Nobody hand-edits the PNGs.
- States, from the reference's second row: `idle` 2 frames 2 fps (breath); `looking` 2 frames 2 fps (hand at the brow); `working` 4 frames 8 fps (walking, speed lines in primary); `success` 4 frames 6 fps (sword up, sparks in accent), once; `blocked` 2 frames 2 fps (`!` in accent, blinking); `alert` 2 frames 4 fps (sword up and `!`); `sleep` 3 frames 2 fps (`z Z` in accent rising). `splash`, from the first row: 7 frames, init, draw (sparks), raise, turn, back, front, ready, once at 6 fps; the last frame holds (amended 2026-09-04, rename: the word under it is gone, the splash carries no wordmark).
- Rendering: PNG sheets drawn with `FilterQuality.none` at integer scales only, ×2 (96 px) in the project summary card (amended 2026-09-03: ×3 is taller than the card's icon slot), ×4 (192 px) on empty states and the splash, never fractional, never bilinear. One `Sprite` widget in `lib/design/sprite.dart`: state name in, manifest read once, frames advanced on a `Ticker`, loops or holds the last frame, stops ticking when not visible.
- Placements (user ruling, 2026-09-03: "the mascot is primarily Dazzle's identity, not an agent-status indicator"): the launcher icon, a static idle frame; the splash on cold start, the seven frames once ending on the idle pose, no wordmark under it (amended 2026-09-04), the one place the animation is prominent; the project detail's summary/icon position, a static idle frame; the AGENTS route's empty state, the idle loop at ×4 with `DAZZLE_` under it, shown when no host is set or when every host answered with zero agents (amended 2026-09-03: `noHost` alone was unreachable, Home hides `VIEW ALL` exactly then; a failing host is not empty, its reason line stays and the mascot does not appear). Nothing on Home: no mascot on the AGENTS panel header, nothing around Capture in this section. The mascot is never bound to aggregate agent state. The state sheets ship as reusable assets and the `Sprite` widget accepts any of them, but no surface opts into `working`, `blocked`, `alert`, `success`, `looking` or `sleep` yet; each later surface asks for its state by name. Agent rows keep 39.30's vendor marks; the reference draws the skeleton there, but the row identifies the vendor.
- Character: one canonical base grid (`design/sprites/mascot/base.txt`) reconstructed by hand from the reference's top-left figure (`design/references/concept/dazzle-mascot-base-crop.png`), never the generated pixels taken literally. Every frame of every state is a derivation of that base with the same silhouette and proportions: the same character turning, moving and reacting, the way the Ghostty mascot does, not a set of independent illustrations.
- Launcher icon (amended 2026-09-03 after the versionCode 24 review, user: "zoom out in the app icon"; "exactly ×16" was arithmetic that never fit a 108 dp canvas): the `idle` first frame on 39.8's ground, centred, adaptive foreground and background as 39.8, file names unchanged so nothing in `android/` moves. Size: the figure's bounding box (41 source rows) fits inside a 58 dp box centred on the 108 dp canvas, which keeps it clear of every launcher mask shape with 4 dp to spare inside the 66 dp safe zone. Per density the scale is the largest integer that meets that: mdpi ×1, hdpi ×2, xhdpi ×2, xxhdpi ×4, xxxhdpi ×5. Nearest-neighbour only, one render per density from the source grid, never a resample of another density's PNG. Nothing of the figure is clipped by the circle, squircle or rounded-square masks; the monochrome layer is the foreground's alpha.
- Not built: the reference's aux icon tiles (today's `hl-*` set stays; kinds that do not exist yet wait for their feature), sound.
- Ownership: artist `design/sprites/`, `design/tools/`, `assets/sprites/`, `assets/icon/`; builder `lib/design/sprite.dart`, the splash route, the bindings. Regression tests: the manifest parses and every listed sheet exists with `frames × 48` width; frame index over elapsed time for a loop and a hold; integer scale choice from the available height; the state mapping table from snapshots, including the 3 s holds.
- Version bumps with the build that ships it.

### 39.34 An ACCESS tab (hosts and keys); the screen leaves AGENTS; keys v2 (user, 2026-09-03: "it's embeded in the agents section when it's a different feature… I should have another tab called 'hosts' were I should be able to see all the registered machines and some metadata… its final goal is to display machines stuff. There we can allow the user to have access to their VNCs with a preview too, a preview for the last frame that he has seen and even blurry, not too much, just enough"; "in the keyboard I noticed that there's a check that in another VNC client is just a enter key which is more comfy than an 'ENTER' button in the screen… [AVNC] offer more keys, I don't use PgUp, PgDown and Home… ask the designer to create app-compatible ones for the keyboard, win key (design it like the command button of mac keyboards) and arrows… For now we're fine if we just provide the ones for toggling the bottom toolbar and other for display or not the keyboard… it should be hidable… the 'live' label is not needed too")
- Tab: the nav bar gains a third destination, `ACCESS`, after `PROJECTS`. Route `/ ACCESS`, the 38 breadcrumb and word. It was going to be `HOSTS`; the user (2026-09-03) wants the ssh keys on the same tab, so the word covers both: the machines and what gets into them. Code names the feature `access`. Icon: the artist's `hl-hosts` (a machine, distinct from `hl-device` and `hl-connection` in the drawer of the real bar); `hl-device` stands in until it lands. Amends 37's "two tabs" and 39.26.
- Body: one Panel per registered host, 39.28's order, each with its own header strip: the host name, and `EDIT` on the right, which pushes the 39.28 form for that host. Rows in the body, 38.4 label style: `ADDRESS` `<address>:<port>`, `USER`, `KEY` (the key's name), `INTEGRATIONS` (the ON ones as mono tokens, `HERDR` `VNC`, or `NONE`), and when `HERDR` is on a `STATE` row carrying the host's latest 39.20 outcome as the panel there would say it (`3 agents · 1 blocked`, or the reason line in its colour). `ADD HOST` as the last text action after the host panels, or alone when there is none, the same route SYS opened. Nothing here edits in place; the form is the one editor.
- Keys: under the hosts, one `KEYS` Panel in the 39.21 block's shape: a row per key, name over the shortened fingerprint, tap pushes the key detail; `ADD KEY` as its last text action, the 39.21 form. Hosts come first because they are what the tab is for; SYS put keys first only because a host cannot be saved without one, and that rule now lives in the form. With the tab holding both, SYS drops its `[ KEYS ]` and `[ HOSTS ]` blocks: `[ STATIC ]`, `[ ALERTS ]`, `[ BUILD ]` remain, and `[ ALERTS ]` keeps its `NO HOST` state.
- Preview tile: when the host has `VNC` on, a tile under the rows, full body width, aspect of the last frame (16:9 when there is none), black ground. It draws the last frame seen on that host's screen, blurred just enough that the layout reads and the text does not: the frame is stored at 320 px wide and drawn with a gaussian blur of sigma 2 at that size, nothing more. Over the tile's bottom-right corner, `SCREEN >` in the affordance style; the whole tile is the hit. No frame yet: the black ground and `NO FRAME YET` centred, mono 11 muted, still tappable. The frame is written when the screen route leaves and every 30 s while it is live, downscaled with area averaging, PNG, `screens/<host id>.png` in the app support directory, one file per host, replaced in place, deleted with the host. It survives restarts.
- The way in moves: 39.32's `SCREEN >` on Home's AGENTS panel header and its picker sheet go. The tile is the only way to a host's screen. The AGENTS panel and route are agents again and nothing else.
- Screen header: the `LIVE` word goes. While live the breadcrumb `/ <HOST> SCREEN` stands alone; `CONNECTING` and the 39.32 reason lines with `RETRY` stay, they say something. `KEYS` in the breadcrumb goes; the rail below replaces it.
- Rail: a vertical Panel on the left edge of the framebuffer area, vertically centred, over the picture, two 44 px icon toggles stacked: keyboard (`hl-keyboard`, shows or hides the soft keyboard) and keys (`hl-keys`, shows or hides the strip). Ink `--fg-dim`, accent while the thing it toggles is up. The two are independent: strip without keyboard (the AVNC frame the user showed), keyboard without strip, both, neither. Closing the keyboard by the system back gesture or the IME's own hide leaves the strip where it was. The rail stays in landscape on the same edge. The rail hides on a horizontal drag to the left over it (user, 2026-09-03, on a build with a fixed rail: "I can't hide it and display it with gestures like move to left to hide and to right to show"): it slides off the left edge and leaves an 8 × 44 dp handle at its vertical centre, `--fg-dim` fill with a 1 dp `surface-primary` keyline so it separates from any picture under it (the first build's 6 dp `--fg-dim` sliver vanished over a light frame). The hidden rail keeps the footprint the shown rail had (left edge to the rail's right border, 44 dp tall) as its hit region, transparent except for the handle: a drag to the right starting anywhere in it, or a tap on it, slides the rail back. The region reaches past Android's back-gesture edge zone, which swallows a drag that starts on the sliver itself (first build, deviation 1). Pointer drags starting inside that patch are lost to the rail, hidden or shown, and nowhere else on the picture. Shown on every route entry; the hidden state lives with the route and is not persisted. Hiding the rail leaves the strip and the keyboard where they were. No timed auto-hide. The `DONE` key goes because the rail closes what it opened.
- Strip v2: two rows of 44 px on a six-column grid, the AVNC arrangement the user pointed at (2026-09-03: "it offers two rows, not everything in just one"). Top row `ESC` `TAB` `SHIFT` · `↑` `END`, bottom row `SUPER` `CTRL` `ALT` `←` `↓` `→`, so the arrows form an inverted T in columns four to six and the fourth cell of the top row stays empty (AVNC keeps `HOME` there; not offered). Columns share the width equally; the strip's own toggles (AVNC's keyboard and close cells at the left) are not repeated, the rail carries them. Replaces the one-row list and 39.32's list; 39.32's typing rule (each character an X keysym press and release) is unchanged. `SHIFT` joins the sticky modifiers, same one-key arming as `CTRL` and `ALT`. `SUPER` draws the artist's `hl-super` glyph, the ⌘-like mark the user asked for, with no word; the four arrows draw `hl-arrow-left/up/down/right`; the rest stay mono 11 tracked words. `ENTER` leaves the strip: the keyboard's own return key is Return (below). `HOME`, `PGUP` and `PGDN` are not offered (user: "I don't use"). The strip sits where 39.32 put it, above the keyboard when the keyboard is up, at the bottom edge when it is not.
- Return from the keyboard: the hidden field takes `TextInputType.multiline` with `TextInputAction.newline`, so Gboard shows its ↵ key instead of the checkmark that sent nothing; a `\n` in the field is Return, sent as one press and release of `XK_Return`, never as text. The field keeps resetting itself after each character as 39.32 had it.
- Icons, artist: `hl-hosts`, `hl-keyboard`, `hl-keys` (a strip of keys, distinct from `hl-keyboard` at 22 px), `hl-super`, `hl-arrow-left`, `hl-arrow-up`, `hl-arrow-down`, `hl-arrow-right`, in the `hl-*` set's stroke rules and pipeline, 22 px in the strip and 20 px in the nav bar, the size the nav bar already draws `hl-home` and `hl-projects` at (first build, deviation 5). Judged in the real drawer next to `hl-device`, `hl-connection`, `hl-back` and `hl-forward` (the arrows must not read as back/forward).
- Regression tests: a `\n` from the IME yields one `XK_Return` press and release and no text keysym; `SHIFT` arms exactly one key; the strip lays out two rows of six cells with `↑` over `↓`; the rail toggles change strip and keyboard visibility independently and the strip survives the keyboard closing; a left drag over the rail hides it and leaves the handle, a right drag starting in the hidden rail's footprint beyond a 24 dp system gesture inset shows it, and neither touches the strip, the keyboard or the pointer stream; the preview writer downsamples a framebuffer to 320 px wide and the tile reads it back; the ACCESS route lists every host with its integration tokens, then every key with its name, and shows a tile only for `VNC` hosts; SYS renders no key or host block; deleting a host removes its frame file; Home's AGENTS header carries no `SCREEN` any more.
- Not built, waiting for the user's word: a timed auto-hide of the rail, more keys, a live thumbnail, the host detail as its own route.
- Ownership: artist `design/`, `assets/icons/`; builder A `lib/`, tests. Version bumps with the build that ships it.

### 39.35 Paths: a drop sent to an agent in a registered directory (user, 2026-09-03: "sería como los 'paths' más comunes… programar tareas para los agents, así luego con la ayuda de herdr se puede crear un nuevo 'space' dentro de los paths registrados y con el agent asignado, siempre se le pasa la flag de 'claude --dangerously-skip-permissions' y 'codex --yolo'… los drops que vaya anotando vayan directamente a donde deben cuando lo amerite, lo normal sería mandarlo directamente… lo de la rutina para una próxima versión, por ahora quiero ver el MVP funcional")
- A path is a host, an absolute directory on it, an agent kind (`claude` or `codex`) and a label, the directory's last segment unless the user types one. Table `paths` (`id`, `host_id`, `directory`, `agent`, `label`, `created_at`); a host's paths go with the host. `~` is allowed at the start of the directory and the host expands it.
- Editor, MVP: the 39.28 host form gains `[ PATHS ]` under `[ INTEGRATIONS ]`, drawn only while `HERDR` is on. One row per path: `<LABEL>` with the 39.30 agent mark, the directory as the mono subtitle, trailing `×` removes; `ADD PATH` last, opening an inline three-field editor in the same panel: `DIRECTORY` (required), `AGENT` (a two-way toggle, `claude` / `codex`), `LABEL` (optional). Tap on a row edits it in place. Saved with the host under 39.28's rules, so leaving the form with unsaved paths asks first. When 39.34's ACCESS tab lands, the rows move there (a later section says how); the data model does not change.
- Send: the drop quick view (39.9: `MOVE` alone) gains a `SEND` footer block when at least one path exists on a reachable-looking host (a path exists; reachability is not checked before the tap). Tap opens the 39.5-style picker of paths, rows `<HOST> / <LABEL>` with the agent mark; choosing one sends and the picker becomes the state sheet: `SENDING`, then `SENT · <pane id>` and a `CLOSE` block, or a reason line with `RETRY`. Only drops in this section: tasks and goals have no `SEND`.
- The prompt is the drop's title as typed, then a blank line and its links one per line when it has any. No template, no system prompt, no agent flags but the two fixed ones below.
- Transport, the fifth gate verb: the app runs `agent spawn <kind> <directory>` over the 39.31 connection and writes the prompt to the channel's stdin, then EOF. The gate: `<kind>` in `claude|codex`, else `denied: kind`; the directory, after `~` expansion, must exist, else `denied: dir`. Then `herdr workspace create --cwd <directory> --label <basename> --no-focus` (the root pane is `.result.root_pane.pane_id`), `herdr agent start <basename> --kind <kind> --pane <pane> -- --dangerously-skip-permissions` for claude and `-- --yolo` for codex, then `herdr agent prompt <pane> <text>`. Stdout on success: one line, the pane id, exit 0; `agent start` and `prompt` failures pass their exit code and stderr through and the gate closes the workspace it just made. The app waits 45 s (start's own readiness timeout is 30 s) and reads: exit 0 with a pane line as sent; `denied: dir` as `no such directory`; other `denied` as `gate refused`; non-zero as `spawn failed`; the deadline as `spawn timed out`; 39.20's connection reasons as they are. The gate script lives in the user's dotfiles as 39.31 says; the verb ships with it.
- Result: table `dispatches` (`id`, `item_id`, `path_id`, `pane_id`, `sent_at`), one row per successful send, written only on exit 0 with a pane line. The quick view of a sent drop shows a `[ SENT ]` panel above the links: one line per dispatch, `<HOST> / <LABEL> · <AGENT> · <pane id> · <time>`, newest first. Its row on Home and in a project carries a mono `SENT` tag at the trailing edge, `--fg-dim`, in the position the `isNext` arrow takes on tasks. The drop stays where it is and stays open: whether a sent drop should leave DROPS, or be filed under the project the path belongs to, waits for the user's word. A RECENT entry is written: `sent "<title>" to <HOST> / <LABEL>`.
- The AGENTS panel needs nothing new: the pane appears on the next 39.20 snapshot as any other agent, and 39.31 alerts on it when it blocks.
- Regression tests: the exact command string and stdin bytes for a send; the four gate answers and the deadline map to their lines; a dispatch row is written only on exit 0 with a pane id; `SEND` is absent with no path and present with one; the `[ PATHS ]` block is absent while `HERDR` is off; deleting a host deletes its paths and their dispatches keep the drop untouched; the title-and-links prompt shape.
- Not built, next version or on the user's word: routines and scheduled sends (user: "para una próxima versión"), sending tasks or goals, editing the prompt before it goes, choosing flags, `SEND` from the selection bar or the capture bar, closing or reading the spawned pane from the app.
- Ownership: builder B (`lib/platform/herdr_client.dart`, data, the form block, the quick view block), the gate verb in dotfiles by the orchestrator, deployed by the user. Version bumps with the build that ships it.

### 39.36 A host's shell inside the app: ssh sessions as tabs (user, 2026-09-03: "proceed with the ssh integration, multiple sessions like tabs should be available, don't try anything weird in the entrypoint")
- What it is: a terminal on the host over its own ssh connection (39.21 key, 39.29 port), the remote's login shell, several at once. No integration toggle: ssh is what a host already is, so every registered host offers it. Nothing new on the host side except the key line: `restrict` refuses pty allocation, so the nyx line gains `pty` beside `port-forwarding,permitopen=…` (dotfiles `user.nix`, the user's rebuild). Until then nyx answers `shell refused`.
- Entry point, the user's rule: the session is `shell` on the ssh channel with a pty of type `xterm-256color` at the view's columns and rows, and nothing else. No command, no `exec`, no env beyond what the pty implies, no rc injection, no tmux or herdr attach, no wrapper. What sshd starts for the user is what runs.
- Way in: on ACCESS, every host panel gains a `SHELL >` affordance row under the metadata rows, above the preview tile when there is one, the whole row the hit. Pushes `/ <HOST> SHELL`. No nav bar, no capture bar; landscape allowed as 39.32's screen route.
- Header: breadcrumb `/ <HOST> SHELL`; on its right the active session's state: `CONNECTING`, nothing while live, `EXITED <code>` once the shell ended, or a 39.20 reason line: `nyx unreachable`, `nyx unresolved`, `key rejected`, `shell refused` (the channel, pty or shell request was denied), `stream closed`. A reason line and `EXITED` come with `RETRY`, which opens a fresh connection in the same tab; there is no reconnect loop.
- Tabs: a 44 px row under the header, one cell per session numbered `1`…`9` in the order opened, mono tracked, the active one in accent with an accent underline, the others `--fg-dim`; a `+` cell last opens a new session and becomes active, gone at nine; a `×` cell at the right end closes the active session, a 39.13-style sheet `CLOSE SHELL <n>?` while it is live, at once when it has exited or failed. Closing the last tab pops the route. Numbers are not reused within a visit.
- Sessions outlive the route: a store per host keeps every session, its connection, its terminal buffer (10 000 lines of scrollback) and its state, so leaving `/ NYX SHELL` and coming back finds the tabs where they were, still running. Sessions end only by `×`, by the remote shell exiting, or with the process. The route's dispose closes nothing.
- Terminal: `xterm` 4.0.0, one `Terminal` per session, `TerminalView` on black ground with `FontFamilies.mono` at 13, cursor block in accent, foreground text primary, ANSI palette from the tokens for black/white/green (accent) and standard hues for the rest. Bytes in are the channel's stdout and stderr; bytes out are the terminal's output; the view's resize sends a window change; the IME's Return is `\r`.
- Rail and strip: 39.34's rail and strip v2 as they are, over the terminal instead of the framebuffer, same toggles, same drag to hide. The strip's keysyms become bytes: `ESC` `1b`, `TAB` `09`, arrows `1b 5b 41..44`, `END` `1b 5b 46`, `CTRL` sticky turns the next letter into its control byte, `ALT` sticky prefixes it with `1b`, `SHIFT` with an arrow sends the `1;2` form; `SUPER` is drawn dim and does nothing here. Return from the strip or the IME is `0d`, backspace `7f`.
- Regression tests: the exact pty request (type, columns, rows) and the absence of any command or env; stdout bytes land in the terminal and typed bytes reach the channel; a resize is forwarded once per size; the exit code turns into `EXITED <code>`; each failure maps to its line and a refused pty to `shell refused`; the strip mapping table above, including `CTRL` `c` → `03` and `ALT` `x` → `1b 78`; the store keeps a session across a route dispose and a `×` closes exactly one; the tab row numbers, the `+` cap at nine, the last close pops; `SHELL >` present on every host panel.
- Not built, waiting for the user's word: copy beyond the IME's own selection, sessions surviving an app restart, one shared connection per host, a command field, font size, `HOME` `PGUP` `PGDN`, port knocking or agent forwarding.
- Ownership: builder A `lib/platform/shell/` (the transport behind the seam the orchestrator wrote in `lib/platform/shell/shell_connection.dart`, the byte mapping, their tests); builder B `lib/features/shell/`, the session store, the ACCESS row, `pubspec.yaml`, their tests. One gate, one commit. Version bumps with the build that ships it.

### 39.37 The shell after Moshi and Termius: a persistent key bar, session cards with live thumbnails, the shell's title (user, 2026-09-03, nine shots of the two ssh apps they use most: "Esas son las dos apps de SSH que más he estado usando hasta ahora, ¿crees que puedas tomar alta inspiración de esas?")
- What is taken, and from where. Moshi: a `SESIONES` surface on the home with one card per running session showing a live miniature of its terminal, captioned `host: title`; the header naming the session by the title the shell set (`nyx: dazzle`); a key bar that is always there. Termius: the same bar, `Tab · shift tab · Esc · Ctrl · ← ↑ ↓` with the keyboard toggle pinned at its right end, scrolling when the phone is too narrow. What is not taken: Moshi's `Tmux / Herdr / Recientes` attach picker and its `Omitir`, because 39.36's entry point stands, and the user's own nyx login shell already draws that menu (`[h] herdr [t] tmux [z] zsh`) host-side where it belongs; the terminal as a bottom sheet, because in Dazzle a route plus back is the same gesture; the FAB; Termius's light theme. Amends 39.36's rail-and-strip and header bullets; everything else in 39.36 stands.
- Key bar: `/ <HOST> SHELL` loses 39.34's rail and strip. In their place a 44 px bar at the bottom of the route, always drawn, above the IME when it is open, landscape included. Cells in order: `CTRL`, `ALT`, `ESC`, `TAB`, `←`, `↑`, `↓`, `→`, `END`, `PASTE`; the artist's strip v2 marks for the arrows, words in the strip's mono; each cell at least 44 wide; the row scrolls horizontally when it does not fit, and the keyboard toggle (strip v2's mark) is pinned at the right end outside the scroll. `CTRL` and `ALT` are sticky and one-shot exactly as the strip's: accent while armed, applied to the next key or typed character, then cleared. `PASTE` reads the clipboard and hands the text to the terminal's own paste, so bracketed paste is honoured when the remote asked for it. The bar's keys go through 39.36's byte table (`stripBytes`), typed characters through `typedBytes` with whatever is armed. No `SUPER`, no `SHIFT`.
- The IME belongs to the terminal now: the `TerminalView` takes text input itself (`hardwareKeyboardOnly` off, a `FocusNode` the route owns); the keyboard toggle focuses and unfocuses it. Return from the IME is `0d`. Because the sticky modifiers must reach characters the IME types straight into the terminal, the armed set lives on the session (`ShellSession.sticky`) and the store applies it where the terminal's output becomes bytes, clearing it after one use; the bar shows and edits that set. Switching tabs switches the armed set with the session.
- Header: the crumb `/ <HOST> SHELL` stays. On its right, while live, the title the shell set through the terminal (OSC 0/2, `Terminal.onTitleChange`), in the panel-header style dimmed, so a session inside herdr reads `dazzle` and a plain prompt reads whatever the shell announces; nothing when no title was set. The state words and the reason lines of 39.36 replace it whenever the session is not live. The store keeps `title` per session and notifies on change.
- `SHELLS` on ACCESS: a panel above the host panels, drawn only while some host has at least one session, titled `SHELLS`. Inside, a horizontal scroll of cards, one per session across all hosts, hosts in ACCESS order then tab order. A card is a live miniature of that session's terminal (`ShellThumbnail`): the visible buffer painted small on black, text primary glyphs, roughly 160×110, hairline border, repainted as output arrives but not more than twice a second; a 6 px state dot in the card's top-left corner in accent while live, `--fg-dim` once exited, warning when failed. Caption under the card: `<HOST> <n>` in the panel-header style and, under it, the shell's title as the mono subtitle when there is one. Tap opens `/ <HOST> SHELL` with that session active. No `+` here; a new shell still starts from the host panel's `SHELL >` row.
- Seam, fixed by the orchestrator so A and B build at once, in `lib/features/shell/`: `ShellBar({required Set<StickyModifier> sticky, required void Function(Set<StickyModifier>) onSticky, required void Function(Uint8List bytes) onBytes, required VoidCallback onPaste, required bool keyboardOpen, required VoidCallback onKeyboard})` and `ShellThumbnail({required Terminal terminal})`, both stateless from the caller's side. `ShellSession` gains `Set<StickyModifier> sticky` and `String? title`.
- Regression tests: the bar sends `ESC` `1b`, `↑` `1b 5b 41`, `END` `1b 5b 46`; `CTRL` armed then `c` typed through the terminal yields `03` and disarms; `ALT` then `←` yields `1b 1b 5b 44`; `PASTE` puts the clipboard text on the wire; the keyboard toggle moves focus to and from the view; the title from an OSC sequence lands on the session and in the header; the header shows the title only while live; the `SHELLS` panel is absent with no session, shows one card per session across two hosts, the dot follows the state, a tap lands on the right tab; the thumbnail paints the buffer's last visible lines; `SUPER` gone from the shell route.
- Not built, waiting for the user's word: a session picker on the host side (the user's login menu does it), session cards on HOME, a `SHIFT` cell, copy from the terminal, font size, closing a session from its card.
- Ownership: builder A `lib/features/shell/shell_bar.dart`, `lib/features/shell/shell_thumbnail.dart`, their tests; builder B `shell_sessions.dart` (sticky, title), `host_shell_screen.dart` (bar in, rail and strip out, IME on the view, title in the header), the `SHELLS` panel on ACCESS, their tests. One gate, one commit, version 1.0.0+32.

### 39.38 The terminal gets the screen: one header row, one shorter key bar (user, 2026-09-03, on the +31 build with the IME open: "siento que el espacio que hay para ver la terminal es importante … el header con lo de nyx shell (el host importa pero no así) ocupa una region importante, lo mismo con el toolbar inferior … apreciaría que fuera de una sola fila y que estén un punto más comprimidos"; and on the two apps: "moshi es muy bueno pero tiene el problema de que no tiene tabs y a veces es fácil cerrar las cosas, que sea un modal sheet es útil pero también la feature de los tabs es un game changer")
- Amends 39.37's header and key bar bullets and 39.36's header. Tabs stay: the user names them the game changer. The sheet is not built: back leaves the route and the session lives on, which is what the sheet's swipe buys in Moshi, and the `SHELLS` cards of 39.37 are the way back in.
- Header, one row of 44: no crumb. Left, the host name in the panel-header style in text primary (the host matters, not the ceremony); then the tab cells `1 … n` and `+` inline, the active one underlined in accent as before; the middle takes the title the shell set, dimmed, ellipsised, or the state word while not live (`CONNECTING`, `EXITED <code>`, the failure kind); `×` at the right. While failed, and only then, a second row of 32 under it: the reason line of 39.36 in warning and `RETRY` at its right. Nothing else above the terminal.
- Key bar, one row of 36 instead of 44: cells sized to their label plus 10 px each side, never narrower than 36, the hit area the full row height; same order, same marks, same scroll, keyboard toggle pinned right. The strip on the screen route is untouched.
- Regression tests, added to 39.37's: the shell route has no `/` crumb; the header is one 44 px row while live and grows the reason row only while failed; the bar measures 36 tall.
- Ownership as 39.37: builder A the bar's height and sizing; builder B the header. Same gate, same commit, 1.0.0+32.

### 39.39 The host is verified, the shell comes back, ACCESS says what answers (user, 2026-09-03, on the gap between the two ssh apps and this one: "creo que le falta bastante a la que tenemos en la app", choosing host key verification, reconnect across a network change, and reachability on ACCESS)
- Three holes, none of them in 39.36-39.38's own lists: the app dials a machine and never checks it is the machine, a shell dies for good when the phone changes network, and ACCESS says nothing about whether anything on it is answering. Amends 39.36's header and states, 39.34's host panel, and every dial site in `lib/platform/`.

**Host key, trust on first use.** Every ssh dial in the app — the shell, the screen's tunnel, the agent poll — verifies the remote's host key against one pinned on the host row, and refuses when it does not match. The pin is `hosts.host_key_type` and `hosts.host_key_fingerprint` (the OpenSSH `SHA256:<base64>` form the transport already computes) with `hosts.host_key_pinned_at`, schema 10, three nullable columns and no backfill: a host registered before this section is unpinned and pins on its next connection. The columns say `host key` and not `ssh` on purpose — they identify the machine, not the thing running on it, and the -rule against transports in the tables is about integrations.
- Unknown key, interactive: the dial pauses and the app asks, in the confirm sheet's shape — header `NEW HOST KEY`, the host name and `<address>:<port>`, the key type and the full fingerprint in mono, wrapped, then `REJECT` and a filled `TRUST`. `TRUST` writes the pin and the dial goes on; `REJECT` fails it as `host key unverified`. Only the routes the user is looking at may ask: the shell opened from `SHELL >` or a `SHELLS` card, the screen route, and `VERIFY` on the host panel.
- Unknown key, unattended: the agent poll, the alert watcher and a reconnect never ask. They refuse an unpinned host and report `host key unverified`, which is a reason line beside the host on AGENTS and on its ACCESS panel, with `VERIFY` next to it — one tap opens the sheet above against a dial of its own.
- Changed key: refused everywhere, always, with no override on the connect path. The line is `host key changed`, in warning. The only way past it is `[ HOST KEY ]` on the host form: the pinned type and fingerprint, when it was pinned, and `FORGET KEY`, which drops the pin behind a `FORGET HOST KEY?` confirm and lets the next connection pin whatever answers. A changed key is a machine reinstalled or a machine in the middle, and the app does not know which; the user does.
- Failure lines, added to the three taxonomies as they stand: `ShellFailureKind.hostKeyUnverified` and `hostKeyChanged`, `VncHostKeyUnverified` and `VncHostKeyChanged`, `HerdrHostKeyUnverified` and `HerdrHostKeyChanged`, `AgentOutcomeKind` the same two. All six word as the two lines above.

**Reconnect across a network change.** A shell that was live and lost its connection reconnects itself. Not mosh: ssh cannot resume, so what comes back is a new login shell on the same tab, above the scrollback the dead one left. That is the honest half of what Moshi buys and the whole of what this section claims.
- The trigger is the kind of failure, not the state it happened in: any dial that ends as `streamClosed` or `unreachable` is waited out, the first one on a tab included. Opening a tab on a host the phone cannot reach yet is the same problem as losing one mid-session, and both are the link's, not the user's. An exit code is the user's own `exit` and stays `EXITED`; `keyRejected`, `shellRefused` and both host key failures are answers, not accidents, and stay failures.
- New state `ShellDropped(attempt)`. The schedule is 1 s, 2 s, 5 s, 10 s, 20 s, 30 s, six attempts, then `ShellFailed` carrying the kind the last dial hit — `nyx unreachable` if that is what it was, not the drop that started the schedule — and the 39.36 reason row. A dial that fails while dropped counts an attempt and takes the next delay; one that fails as anything the trigger excludes stops at once and reports that instead.
- `RETRY` goes, from `EXITED` and from the reason row both. Dialling a host is the app's job and it is now doing it on a schedule; a button that asks the user to do it by hand says the app cannot. What is left in the reason row is the sentence. The way into another shell on a host whose failure was an answer is `+`, a new tab, which is the same gesture as the first one.
- Header, while dropped: `RECONNECTING <n>/6` where the title goes, dimmed, and `STOP` in the affordance style at its right, which parks the session at `ShellFailed(streamClosed)`. The one control on this route, because giving up is a decision and dialling again is not. No reason row while dropped — the row is for a shell that has given up.
- The terminal gets one dim line per reconnect, `-- reconnected --` centred in nothing, written into the buffer before the new shell's first byte, because a fresh prompt appearing under the old one's output with no mark is a lie about what happened.
- Anything armed is cleared and the title is dropped: they belonged to the shell that died. Scrollback is kept. Closing the tab or leaving the app stops the schedule; the route being popped does not.

**ACCESS says what answers.** Two things per host panel, both above the `SHELL >` row.
- `LINK`, a 39.34 metadata row: `—` before the first probe, `PROBING` while one is in flight, `<software> · <n> MS` when the machine answered — `OPENSSH_9.9 · 120 MS`, the software token off the identification string, uppercased — or the failure word in warning: `NO ROUTE`, `UNRESOLVED`, `REFUSED`, `NO BANNER`. The probe is a TCP connection to `<address>:<port>` that reads the identification line and hangs up, 4 s deadline, no key and no handshake. It reads the banner rather than settling for the connection because a port that accepts and then stalls is a tarpit and not a shell — the user has one of those on a LAN address. Run for every host when ACCESS comes to the front, at most once every 30 s per host, and on a tap of the row, which probes that host again whatever the age.
- The `SHELL >` row counts what is already open: `SHELL` alone with none, `SHELL · <n> OPEN` with some, and a 6 px state dot before the word in the 39.37 card's colours, taking the worst state among that host's sessions — warning if any failed, accent if any is live, muted otherwise.
- `VERIFY` sits on the `LINK` row's right end, and only while the host is unpinned or its key changed.
- Regression tests: an unpinned host asks once and the pin is written on `TRUST`; `REJECT` fails as `host key unverified` and writes nothing; a fingerprint that differs from the pin fails as `host key changed` in every one of the three transports and never asks; the poll, the watcher and the send refuse an unpinned host without asking; `FORGET KEY` clears the three columns; a live session dropped as `streamClosed` schedules 1 s and reaches `ShellFailed` after the sixth, a first dial that never lands is waited out the same way, an exit code does not schedule at all, `STOP` parks it, and each reconnect writes one marker line; the header reads `RECONNECTING <n>/6` and carries `STOP` and no `RETRY`, and neither does the reason row; the probe maps a banner, a refusal, a lookup failure and a silent socket to their four lines and is not re-run inside 30 s; the `SHELL >` row counts sessions and the dot takes the worst state.
- Not built, waiting for the user's word: importing an existing private key, a passphrase, password auth, ssh-agent, `ProxyJump`, a `known_hosts` import, reconnect that survives the app being killed, and a probe on any schedule of its own.
- Ownership: one session, `lib/platform/`, `lib/data/`, `lib/features/access/`, `lib/features/shell/`, tests. One gate, one commit, version 1.0.0+33.

### 39.40 `VERIFY` says what it is doing, and a host is asked about where it is added (user, 2026-09-04, on the +33 build: "I tap 'verify' and nothing is displayed…", "at least that tap is not working in nyx but it is working in rose", and "also, the 'verify' should also happen when I add the new ssh key")
- 39.39 shipped the dial and forgot the user in front of it. Two faults, one of them the reason the other was invisible. Amends 39.39's `VERIFY` and the host form.

**The dial outlives the question.** dartssh2 counts the verifier's answer as part of the handshake, so the 10 s handshake deadline was running while the sheet was open and the connection was already dead by the time anyone tapped `TRUST`. The pin still landed — it is written on this side — which is why the machine that answered fast looked like it worked and the one that did not looked broken.
- The dial's deadline is on the machine presenting a key, not on the user reading one: it is armed at the connect and disarmed the moment the key arrives. dartssh2's own is set out of the way. The dial returns once the key has been answered for; authentication is never what it was waiting for.
- A socket that opens and then says nothing is `HostKeySilent`, which is a machine behind a tarpit and not a machine that is down. Both were silence before.

**Every dial says what it is doing.** The `VERIFY` affordance is the whole report, in the row the user tapped: `VERIFYING` while one is out, and the word the dial came back with when it fails — `NO ANSWER` for a socket that never presented a key, `NO ROUTE` for one that never opened, `KEY CHANGED` for a fingerprint that is not the pinned one, `NO KEY` for a host whose private key the keystore no longer has, `FAILED` for the rest — in warning, standing until the next tap. A user who taps `REJECT` said no on purpose and gets `VERIFY` back, not a failure. Nothing is written only to the debug log any more: a handset has no way to read one.

**A host is asked about where it is registered.** `CREATE HOST` and `SAVE` dial the machine themselves when the host has no pin, before the form is left, and put the same `NEW HOST KEY` sheet up. The bar reads `VERIFYING` while that is out. Every failure leaves the host saved and unpinned with `VERIFY` on its panel: a machine that is not answering while a form is being filled in is not a reason to hold the user on it.
- Regression tests: a dial against a socket that accepts and never speaks throws `HostKeySilent` inside the deadline; the row reads `VERIFYING` while a dial is out and `NO ANSWER` after a silent one; creating a host dials once, asks, and `TRUST` writes the pin before the form pops.
- Ownership: one session, `lib/platform/host_key_dial.dart`, `lib/features/access/`, `lib/features/settings/`, tests. One gate, one commit, version 1.0.0+34.

### 39.41 The watcher is told which key the host is trusted on (user, 2026-09-04, on the +34 build: "I tap 'verify', it goes to 'verifying' and returns to 'verify' again")
- 39.40 made the dial report itself and the report was `VERIFY` again, on a host whose key had been pinned for weeks. The affordance was never the fault. Amends 39.40 and the watcher's host payload.

**The pin did not cross into the service.** `encodeWatcherHosts` wrote a host's address, port, user and key id into the store the foreground service reads, and left the pinned host key behind. The service rebuilt every host without one, dialled with a verifier that has nobody to ask, and refused the machine on every tick — a `host key unverified` line, and a herdr connection dropped before authentication once a minute, on a host the user had already stood in front of and trusted.
- The pin travels with the host, and a pin that appears or changes is a different dial: the loop already running under the old one is stopped rather than left refusing the machine.
- The panel's `VERIFY` reads that same poll, which is why the tap could not clear it. The affordance was reporting a verdict about a question the tap had just answered.

**A dial that came back clean is an answer.** A machine that presents the key the app already trusts refuses nothing and asks nothing, and the row now takes that as the answer it dialled for: the affordance goes, whatever the last poll still says. The row's own dial outranks a poll it does not re-run.
- Regression tests: a pinned key survives `encodeWatcherHosts` into `decodeWatcherHosts`; the watcher dials a pinned host with a verifier that accepts that fingerprint and refuses any other; `VERIFY` on a host that is pinned but whose last poll says otherwise dials once and stops offering.
- Ownership: one session, `lib/platform/agent_watcher.dart`, `lib/features/access/access_screen.dart`, tests. One gate, one commit, version 1.0.0+35.

### 39.42 SSH is its own tab, over the sessions already open (user, 2026-09-04: "please create another dedicated tab for ssh, 'access' tab should get renamed to 'hosts'", "it will be on top in the ssh tab and below I should be able to pick one of the shells", "how it is displayed in termius in the overview page… so it is in the 'SSH > Active Sessions' section")
- 39.34 named the tab `ACCESS` while calling it "the registered machines and the keys that get into them", and 39.37 hung the running shells off the bottom of it. Two subjects on one screen: what a machine is, and what is running on it right now. Amends 39.34 and 39.37.

**`ACCESS` is `HOSTS`.** The name 39.34 wanted in the first place, taken all the way through: the directory, the screen class, the route test and the breadcrumb the key and host forms print. Nothing in the code calls this feature `access` any more. Its position in the bar does not move — a user who knows the third tab still finds the same screen under a truer name.

**`SSH` is the fourth destination**, on `hl-connection`, last in the bar. It is Termius's overview and not a terminal: nothing on it is typed into, both of its sections are ways to reach one.
- `ACTIVE SESSIONS` on top, the 39.37 cards moved off `HOSTS` whole — thumbnail, `<HOST> <n>`, the shell's own title, the state dot — one row of them across every host that has a session, in host order. A tab with nothing open has no such section: an empty shelf is worse than the list starting at the top.
- `HOSTS` below it, one row per registered machine: the name, `ssh, <user>` under it, the worst state of its sessions as a dot on the left, `n OPEN` on the right for a host that has any. Tapping one dials — the host's last active tab, or a first session on a machine with none. No host registered reads `NO HOSTS REGISTERED`.

**`HOSTS` keeps the machine.** The host panels, their link state, `VERIFY`, the `SHELL >` row that still counts sessions and takes the worst dot, and the keys. What left is only the card strip.
- `worstShellState`, the state dot and the session card are one definition in `lib/features/shell/`, read by both tabs. A card is not drawn twice in two shapes.
- Regression tests: a host row says `ssh, <user>` and never the address; no session means no `ACTIVE SESSIONS` and a `HOSTS` list regardless; every session is a card, in host order, and its host's row counts them; a card's dot follows its own session's state; a card opens its own host on its own tab and a row with no session opens one; `HOSTS` has no cards and no `SHELLS` panel left on it, and still counts on the `SHELL >` row.
- Ownership: one session, `lib/app/`, `lib/features/hosts/`, `lib/features/shell/`, `lib/features/ssh/`, tests. One gate, one commit, version 1.0.0+36.

### 39.43 A shell that exits clean takes its tab, and one that does not says why (user, 2026-09-04: "when the session is killed with session 0 it is closed, in case of any other status code it should display it in a modal")
- 39.38 gave an ended shell a tab that stays and a header that reads `EXITED <code>`, which treats `exit` and a shell that died the same and puts the code where a user who is not looking at the route never sees it. Amends 39.38.

**Exit 0 closes the tab.** The user typed `exit`, the tab holds nothing they asked to keep, and it goes the moment the code arrives — from the store, so it goes whether or not anybody is on the route. The last tab going takes the route with it, the same pop closing the last one by hand does. That pop has one owner now: the store's listener, which every way of losing the last tab passes through.

**Every other code is a sheet.** `SHELL <n> EXITED <code>`, one at a time, over the terminal the shell died in, with `CLOSE` and `KEEP` — `KEEP` filled, because the output under the sheet is the only place the shell wrote what went wrong. A connection that went away without a code reads `EXITED —`, the word the header already uses. A tab is announced once: the sheet is the moment it ended, and the header goes on saying it afterwards.
- Regression tests: exit 0 drops the tab and puts up nothing, and the last one pops the route; exit 3 puts up the sheet, `KEEP` leaves the tab reading `EXITED 3` and `CLOSE` drops it; a connection closed with no code reads `EXITED —`; a tab announced once is not announced again on the next notify.
- Ownership: one session, `lib/features/shell/`, tests. One gate, one commit, version 1.0.0+37.

### 39.44 SSH sessions survive a restart (user, 2026-09-04: "SSH sessions should survive app reboots")
- 39.38 keeps a host's tabs for as long as the app runs, in a store nothing disposes. The process dying takes them anyway: the tab set lives in memory and the shells live in sockets, so a swipe from the recents list comes back on `NO SESSIONS` with the work the user left mid-flight nowhere on screen.

**What is written down is the tab, not the shell.** An ssh channel dies with the process that held it; nothing on the phone can keep one warm across a launch, and a session that claimed to be the same shell would be lying about the state on the remote. What survives is the row: host, number, and whether it was the tab on screen. `tmux` on the far side is what makes the shell itself survive, and that is the remote's job, not this app's.

**Startup dials them back.** `main()` reads the rows before the first frame and opens a tab per row, on the host it was on and under the number it had, so the numbering carries on where it stopped rather than restarting at 1. The dials are not awaited — the boot log is on screen while they run — and the log carries `restore shells <n>`, since it reports real work. Each dial is unattended, so a host whose key was never pinned is refused here as it is on a reconnect. A row whose host was deleted while the app was down is dropped rather than dialled.

**The table follows the tab row.** Opening writes a row and makes it the active one, closing takes it out, switching tabs moves the flag, and a shell that exits clean takes its row with its tab (39.43). A tab left on a failed shell keeps its row: it is dialled again next launch, which is the thing the user wanted the tabs back for.
- Regression tests: open writes and close removes; the active flag follows `activate` and a close that moves it; a second store over the same table comes up on the same tabs, numbers and active tab, one dial each; numbering carries on past the restored tabs; a row on a deleted host is dropped, not dialled; restore runs once however often it is called; a restored dial carries no prompt; the migration lands the table empty and takes writes.
- Ownership: one session, `lib/features/shell/`, `lib/data/`, `lib/main.dart`, tests. One gate, one commit, version 1.0.0+38.

### 39.45 A path is picked, not typed, and the agent is chosen when the drop is sent (user, 2026-09-04: "debería de darme una interfaz más fácil para registrarlos, no decirme que me lo sepa de memoria", "lo del agent seleccionado, debería de ser on-demand", "si quiero añadir puedo agregarlos en masa, ¿no?")
- 39.30 had the host form take a directory as free text and an agent kind beside it, which asks the user to spell a remote path from memory on a phone keyboard and to decide months in advance which CLI works a checkout. Amends 39.30.

**The host answers with its own checkouts.** `agent paths` walks the gate's roots for git checkouts and returns them as a list; `ADD PATH` opens a sheet over the form that shows it, filtered by a field that matches anywhere in the path. `.git` is matched by name and not by type, so a superproject's submodules are in the list. A gate that does not know the verb answers nothing and the sheet says so, with the free-text field still there as the escape hatch — an older host still sends.

**Registration is a multiple pick.** The sheet checks rows off and closes on `ADD n`, because a host is registered once and has thirty checkouts on it, not one. A path already registered is kept out of the list. A tap on an existing row reopens the same sheet in single mode and re-points that row.

**A row is one line.** The last two segments of the directory — the owner and the repository, which is what tells two checkouts of the same name apart. No label line, no directory line, and no agent mark: a path answers for a directory and nothing else.

**The agent and the model are the send's.** `paths.agent` moves to `dispatches.agent`, with a `dispatches.model` beside it (schema v12; every dispatch already made inherits the kind its path carried). Choosing a target in a drop opens a step that asks the host what `kind` will accept — `agent models <kind>`, codex's own bundled slugs and claude's aliases — and the send carries the slug, or `-` for the agent's own default. The sent line says the kind and the model it went out with.

**SEND is not a drop's verb.** An item filed under a project is text an agent can pick up like any other, so the footer offers SEND on both; only a host with no path at all takes the verb away.
- Regression tests: the picker offers what the host answered and filters it; a bulk add registers every checked row and a single tap re-points one; an already-registered path is not offered; the row shows the last two segments; the escape hatch takes a typed path; a v11 dispatch reads back with its path's agent and no model, and `paths` has no `agent` column; the models step lists what the host answered and the send carries the pick; an item with a project still offers SEND.
- Ownership: one session, `lib/features/hosts/`, `lib/features/tasks/`, `lib/features/settings/`, `lib/data/`, the gate, tests. One gate, one commit, version 1.0.0+39.

### 39.45 CLIPS: a crop of the host's screen, kept for the run, pasted into a shell (user, 2026-09-04: "cuando estoy en VNC pueda hacer crop y que se mantenga ahí, en la app, lo mismo podría hacerse para la subida de fotos, no videos ni texto puro, el punto es que luego podré usar eso para hablarle a un agent"; "only RAM and cache I guess, let's ensure it doesn't consume a lot"; "these were meant to be pastable into ssh inputs so for example claude code can handle it and put '[Image 1]', the same for codex cli")
- The name is not settled (user: "CLIPS if you want it but is a pretty ambiguous feature so add a '?'"). `CLIPS` is what the app says and what the code calls it until the user rules otherwise. `Buffer` is not the word: `Framebuffer` already owns it in `lib/platform/vnc/`, three files deep, and a second meaning there would read as the same thing.
- A clip is one still image held for as long as the app runs. Two sources and no others: a rectangle cut out of the live framebuffer on the SCREEN route, and a photo from the gallery. No video, no plain text, no clipboard capture. Nothing about a clip reaches drift: it is not an attachment, it has no row, and it does not survive a launch.
- Cutting one: the 39.34 rail gains a third 44 px toggle under the two it has, `hl-crop`, which arms crop mode. Armed, the picture stops taking pointer events for the host and takes a drag instead, which draws a rect over it in the accent, dimming what falls outside; the rail's other toggles and the strip are inert while it is armed. Releasing leaves the rect on screen with `CLIP` and `CANCEL` in the affordance style at its lower edge; a fresh drag anywhere replaces it. `CLIP` maps both corners through `ScreenTransform.toFramebuffer`, so the cut is in host pixels under fit and under zoom alike, clamps them to the framebuffer, refuses a rect under 8 px on either side, copies the pixels into a `PreviewFrame` beside `downsampleFrame`, and encodes it with the `encodePng` the preview tile already uses. Nothing is downscaled: a crop of a terminal is read by an agent, and filter 0 with deflate over flat glyphs is small already. Crop mode disarms on `CLIP`, on `CANCEL`, and on leaving the route.
- Photos: the tray's `ADD PHOTO` opens the gallery through `ImageAttachments.pick`, 39.9's picker, and copies the file into the cache with the extension it had. A photo is never re-encoded and never decoded to add it.
- Where a clip lives: `<temporary directory>/clips/<id>.<ext>`, the cache Android may take back on its own. `main()` deletes that directory before the first frame, next to 39.44's restore, which is what makes "runtime only" true across a kill the user did not see. In memory the app holds the record and nothing else — id, file, pixel size, source, host it was cut from, the moment. No thumbnail is kept: the tray draws `Image.file` with a `cacheWidth` of 128, so Flutter decodes at tray size and never holds the full picture. The budget is 8 clips and 24 MB in the directory, whichever binds first; a ninth clip, or one that crosses the ceiling, deletes the oldest until it fits. A clip is dropped by hand from the tray, and dropping the last one takes the directory with it.
- The tray: a sheet, `[ CLIPS ]`, a grid of the clips newest first, each with its pixel size in mono 11 under it and a `×`. It opens from one cell added to the 39.38 key bar, `CLIPS` with the count beside it once there is one. Always drawn (user, 2026-09-04: the tray "should open a modal to help us to also import from gallery"): the tray is the only way a photo comes in, and that has to be reachable before anything has been cropped. `ADD PHOTO` is the last action in the sheet.
- Landing a clip on a host: a second exec channel on the same ssh client, never the pty, whose bytes a pty would translate. `mkdir -p /tmp/dazzle && cat > /tmp/dazzle/<id>.<ext>`, the bytes to stdin, EOF, exit 0. No sftp: a plain exec channel is what this app already opens and it works on a host with the subsystem off. The bytes go up on every paste, not once per host: `/tmp` is cleared by a reboot the app never hears about, and a path the agent is then told to read would be a lie; a crop is a few hundred kilobytes and a photo is capped, so the upload is cheaper than being wrong. `/tmp` is the user's ruling and the file is left there; the host's own reboot is the cleanup.
- Pasting is the clipboard, on every host that has one. Neither agent reads the picture off the terminal: codex's TUI is ratatui over crossterm and takes the image through `arboard`, Claude Code's `Ctrl+V` is the same shape, and what crossterm sees either way is a key. Over ssh the agent runs on the host, so the clipboard that matters is the host's, and the app writes it directly: upload, set the host's clipboard to the file as `image/png`, then one `0x16` into the pty. Both agents answer `[Image #1]`.
- Four clipboard backends, one per display server, all first class. **Wayland**: `wl-copy --type image/png` under `setsid`, since the process that owns a wayland selection must outlive the channel that started it. **X11**: `xclip -selection clipboard -t image/png`, likewise detached, `xsel --clipboard --input --mime-type image/png` when `xclip` is absent. **macOS**: `osascript -e 'set the clipboard to (read (POSIX file "<path>") as «class PNGf»)'`, which is the only way to put a picture on `NSPasteboard` from a shell. **None**: the host has no display server, and there is no clipboard to write. Windows hosts are not addressed; the probe reports them as none.
- Two routes for every exec, settled by the probe and never guessed at a paste (user, 2026-09-04: the row read `probe exit 2 · denied: sh -c 'printf …`). A key line under a forced command hands every exec to that command and runs none of the text, which is exactly what `dazzle-gate` does on the user's hosts: it takes a pty with no command as the SHELL tab and the `agent` verbs, and refuses the rest with `denied: <command>` and exit 2. So the gate grows a `clip` family — `clip probe`, `clip put <name>`, `clip copy <name>` — that does behind each verb what the scripts below do, with `<name>` held to `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+.png` and the file always under `/tmp/dazzle`; its `probe` prints in the script's shape, so one parser reads both. The app asks the gate first: an answer that exits 0 and opens with `os=` is a gate and every command after it goes as a verb; anything else — a login shell's `not found`, a gate too old for the verb — and the scripts run under `sh`. A gate that knows no `clip` is reported off the `sh` attempt, `NONE · probe exit 2 · denied: sh -c …`, which is the line that led here. The gate lives in the user's dotfiles, `system/modules/programs/security/dazzle-gate.sh`, and the change there ships with a host rebuild the user does.
- The probe, once per host per connection, cached on the connection and re-run when it comes back. One exec channel answers all of it: `uname -s`; `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, defaulted to `/run/user/$(id -u)`, and the first `wayland-*` socket in it; `DISPLAY` and `XAUTHORITY`; and `command -v` for `wl-copy`, `wl-paste`, `xclip`, `osascript`, `timeout`. An ssh exec channel inherits none of a desktop session's environment, so every one of those is read rather than assumed, and the pair the backend needs is carried on the command the paste runs. Wayland is probed one step further: `timeout 1 wl-paste --watch true` is run and its exit code and stderr kept. `--list-types` was the first idea and is wrong — wl-clipboard's source exits it 0 on an empty selection and 1 on "Nothing is copied", neither of which is about the protocol — while watch mode bails at once with "Watch mode requires a compositor that supports the data-control protocol" and otherwise runs until the timeout kills it. So exit 124 is `wlr-data-control` present, and exit 1 with that line is the compositor lacking it, which is the same protocol extension `arboard` needs to read the selection back. wlroots compositors implement it, GNOME's Mutter does not, and on Mutter the picture goes onto the clipboard correctly and the agent still cannot see it ([codex#24908](https://github.com/openai/codex/issues/24908)). That is upstream and not this app's to fix, so a host that fails this step is a host with no clipboard as far as CLIPS is concerned, and is reported as such rather than being allowed to fail at the moment of a paste.
- The result is a fact about the host and is drawn as one, not hidden in a paste: the 39.34 HOSTS panel gains a `CLIPBOARD` row while the host has a shell open, `WAYLAND` `X11` `MACOS` or `NONE` as mono tokens, and `NONE` carries the reason under it in `--fg-dim` — `no display server`, `no wl-copy`, `no xclip`, `compositor without data-control`. A host whose clipboard is missing a tool it could have is a host the user can fix, which is the point of saying which tool.
- Where there is no clipboard the app types the file's absolute path into the pty at the cursor and a space after it. This is not a lesser version of the feature: it is the documented way in for both agents — Claude Code lists "Provide an image path to Claude. E.g., `Analyze this image: /path/to/your/image.png`" beside drag-and-drop and paste, and codex's input layer takes text and file paths — and on a headless host it is the only way in that exists, since there is no clipboard for anything to paste from. The tray says which of the two ran, `PASTED` or `PATH`, mono 11 for a moment. Neither failing types anything and neither leaves half a paste: the upload must exit 0 before the clipboard command runs, and the clipboard command must exit 0 before the `0x16` goes in.
- Rejected, so nobody builds it later by accident: forwarding the host's wayland socket over `direct-streamlocal@openssh.com` and speaking `wlr-data-control` from Dart. It removes the dependency on `wl-copy` and buys nothing else — it cannot help X11, macOS or a headless host, and it puts a hand-written wayland protocol client in a phone app to save one `command -v`.
- Regression tests: a rect in view coordinates under fit and under zoom cuts the same host pixels; a rect running past the edge is clamped and one under 8 px is refused; the cut pixels round-trip through `encodePng` at the size the header declares; a photo comes in as PNG whatever the gallery handed over, at most 2048 on its long side; the ninth clip drops the first and a clip crossing 24 MB drops until it fits; launch deletes the directory; the upload runs the exact command and writes the exact bytes on an exec channel; the probe's output maps to each of the four backends, a `wl-paste --watch` that exits anything but 124 maps to none with the data-control reason, and it runs once per connection; each backend runs its exact command with the environment the probe found; a successful clipboard write puts exactly one `0x16` into the pty and no path; a none host types the path and a trailing space and no `0x16`; an upload that fails, and a clipboard command that exits non-zero, both type nothing; the `CLIPBOARD` row carries each token and each reason; the key bar cell reads `CLIPS` with no clip and carries the count with some; a probe the host refused reads `probe exit N` and the first line the host said; behind the gate the three verbs run in order with the bytes on `put`, and nothing under `sh`; crop mode swallows the pointer and the strip while armed and releases both when it disarms; leaving SCREEN disarms it.
- Not built, waiting on the user's word: a clip on a 39.35 send (`-i <path>` on the `agent start` line, a gate verb change and a host rebuild), clips as task attachments, more than one clip pasted at once, editing or rotating a clip, restoring the host's clipboard after a paste, Windows hosts, and anything text.
- Ownership: one session, `lib/features/screen/`, `lib/features/shell/`, `lib/features/hosts/`, `lib/platform/`, `lib/application/`, `lib/main.dart`, tests, and the `clip` verbs in the dotfiles gate. One gate, one commit here and one there. Version bumps with the build that ships it.

### 39.46 A crop is saved or held, ATTACH replaces CLIPS, photos go straight in (user, 2026-09-04: "it is kinda weird to keep holding some images"; "the user should be able to select wether to save it for the phone gallery or to hold it in memory for the ssh"; "at the end of the crop the user can read as a temp system message what happened with the cropped content"; "rename it to 'attach'? it should offer two options with a little '!', one is to pick many images and the other from the memory")
- The two verbs under the box are `SAVE` and `HOLD`, both in the accent; `CANCEL` is gone from the edge, since a fresh drag replaces the box and the rail toggle leaves the mode. `SAVE` writes the PNG to the phone's gallery under an album `Dazzle`, through MediaStore via `gal`; Android 10 and later need no permission for the app's own media, and the manifest carries `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` capped at API 28 for the rest, asked for on the first save. `HOLD` is 39.45's path into memory, unchanged. Never both: a saved crop is not held and a held one is not saved.
- The temp system message is the breadcrumb's state slot, the one `CONNECTING` and a reason line use while not live: for two seconds after the verb it reads `SAVED · 240×120`, `HELD · 240×120`, or `SAVE FAILED · <reason>` / `HOLD FAILED · <reason>`, in the panel header style, then the breadcrumb stands alone again. Nothing is drawn over the picture for it.
- Lifetime is the app run, as 39.45 has it. A clip cropped on one host may be attached on another, so the shell session is the wrong owner for it.
- The key bar cell reads `ATTACH`, and `ATTACH!` while memory holds anything; the count is not on the bar. The sheet it opens is `[ ATTACH ]`: `PHOTOS` first, an action block that opens a multi-pick of the gallery and attaches every picture picked, in order, each at most 2048 on its long side and made a PNG; then a rule and `HELD`, the tiles of 39.45 or `NOTHING HELD`, a tap attaching that one. Photos are never held: they go in and are forgotten, so `ADD PHOTO` is gone. `PASTE` is off the bar too (user, 2026-09-04: "i don't need a paste button in the toolbar"; "you forgot to rm the paste button"): the phone's text clipboard reaches the terminal through the IME, and 39.37's cell list is read less that one.
- Many at once: one gate `put`, one `copy`, one `0x16` per picture, with 300 ms between pictures because the agent reads the clipboard on the keystroke and the next write has to wait for that read. The sheet reads `ATTACHING 2 OF 5` while it runs and `PASTED · 5` or `PATH · 5` at the end for a moment; a failure stops the run where it is and leaves its line on the sheet.
- The login shell behind the gate carries the desktop's display (user, 2026-09-04, codex under a plain zsh: `Failed to paste image: clipboard unavailable: … X11 server connection timed out because it was unreachable`). An ssh login has none of the compositor's environment, so an agent started in the SHELL tab found no `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, and arboard, which picks Wayland only on that variable, fell to X11 and a socket it could not reach; the picture was on the Wayland clipboard the whole time. The gate's pty branch now runs the same discovery `clip probe` does and exports `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` and one display before it execs the login shell — `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` when a socket is found, `DISPLAY` only otherwise — so an agent in that shell reads the selection the phone wrote. One and not both because Claude Code's Linux reader runs `xclip` first and `wl-paste` only when that fails, and on a Wayland desktop the X11 clipboard is Xwayland's bridge, which holds the selection only while an X11 window has focus; with `DISPLAY` set that reader would answer from whichever window the desktop happened to be on. A multiplexer attached from the desktop never had the problem, which is why it went unseen. Ships with a host rebuild, as 39.45's verbs do; a host with no gate is on its own shell's environment.
- Regression tests: the bar has no `PASTE` cell; `SAVE` and `HOLD` each hand back the same box in framebuffer pixels with their target; a fresh drag replaces the box and neither verb fires; the notice lines carry the size and the verb; the bar reads `ATTACH` unmarked and `ATTACH!` with something held and no count either way; a photo goes as bytes under a fresh `<uuid>.png` name and is not held.
- Ownership: one session, the same tree as 39.45 plus `lib/platform/gallery.dart` and the manifest. Version bumps with the build that ships it.

### 39.47 The terminal's type is pinched to size, and the size is kept (user, 2026-09-04, on the +43 build with a shell full of prose: "the text can be a little big so i cannot take advantage of all my screen, i should be able to scale up/down i guess so the text buffer that can go here can be bigger")
- A two-finger pinch on the terminal scales its type: spread to grow, close to shrink, live while the fingers are down, rounded to whole points and held between 8 and 24. 13 stays the default, the size every build so far has drawn. Columns and rows follow the size the way they follow a rotation: the view re-measures, the terminal resizes and the host is told, so a smaller face is more columns and rows, not more margin.
- One size for every shell on every host, kept in the settings table under `shell_font_size` as the number, written when the fingers lift and read on the next open. It is the user's reading distance, not a property of a host or a tab, so it is not per anything. No row for it on SETTINGS: the gesture is on the surface it changes and the change is visible while it happens, which is the whole of the feedback.
- One finger stays the terminal's: a one-finger drag scrolls the buffer as before and a long press still selects. The pinch is taken only once two fingers have moved apart or together past the tap slop, so a scroll that happens to land a second finger is not a resize.
- The phone's font scale still applies on top, as it did: the size is the app's, the scaler is the system's.
- Regression tests: the view opens at 13; a spread grows the size and a close shrinks it; a spread past the ceiling lands on 24 and a close past the floor on 8; the size written to settings is what the next open of the route draws; the route with no settings behind it draws 13.
- Ownership: one session; `lib/features/shell/host_shell_screen.dart`, `lib/application/shell_font.dart`, `lib/application/providers.dart`, `lib/data/repositories/settings_repository.dart` and their tests. Version bumps with the build that ships it.

### 39.48 Hosts use one ordinary SSH key; the forced-command gate is gone (user, 2026-09-04: "what if we just suggest it in the readme or anything else and let's put it exclusively for my system I guess without anything weird so SSH has full access"; "keep it very simple so I can easily integrate it with my mac and use vnc, ssh and more shit")
- The app-generated public key is a plain `authorized_keys` line. It carries no forced command, `restrict`, or `permitopen`. SHELL already gave the key a full login shell, so the gate did not form a security boundary; it only split the same account access into a private verb protocol that every host configuration had to copy. This section retires that protocol and amends the gate routes in 39.45 and 39.46.
- Every non-PTY operation is a quoted script under `sh -c`. Clipboard probe, upload, and copy have one route. Herdr operations carry `HERDR_SESSION=hub` (struck 39.88, 2026-09-08: no session name is the product's; list and watch sweep every running session, and every other command carries the session its pane was listed in): list and watch call `herdr agent list`, read and keys call their Herdr CLI commands, model discovery reads Codex's bundled model JSON or Claude's three aliases, and checkout discovery scans `~/Projects`, `~/.dotfiles`, and `~/src` for `.git` entries.
- SEND is a transaction owned by the app: create a Herdr workspace, parse its pane, start Claude with its one-run bypass settings or Codex with `--yolo`, then send the prompt from stdin. A bad directory creates nothing. A start or prompt failure closes the workspace. A 45-second deadline reports `spawn timed out` and stops the transaction from advancing.
- An agent that answers `agent start` with `agent_not_ready` is up and waiting on its own first-run trust dialog when nobody has opened it in that directory before. The app reads the pane, answers Claude's dialog with `down`, `enter` and Codex's with `enter`, then holds on `herdr agent wait --until idle` for 15 s before the prompt goes. Any other blocked screen, or a wait that expires, is `agent start failed` and closes the workspace. The prompt itself runs `herdr agent prompt --wait --until working --timeout 6000`, so its exit code says whether the agent read the text: Claude reports idle a beat before it accepts input after that dialog and drops what arrives in the beat. A stalled prompt is sent again, three attempts in all, inside the same 45 s deadline. The user chose the directory and the run already bypasses permissions, so the dialog asks nothing the send has not answered.
- The desktop display belongs in the login-shell configuration. On Linux, a shell with no `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` discovers the first socket under `${XDG_RUNTIME_DIR:-/run/user/$UID}` and exports both values. macOS clipboard access needs no display export; `osascript` writes the PNG pasteboard item.
- The repository README documents the plain key, host tools, `hub` session (struck 39.88: no named session), checkout roots, display export, and VNC limit. SCREEN still forwards to `127.0.0.1:5900`, but the client accepts only RFB security type `None`. Apple Screen Sharing does not offer it, so macOS SSH and attachments work while its built-in VNC server does not.
- The user's NixOS and nix-darwin repositories each carry the two plain Dazzle public keys. The NixOS repository deletes `dazzle-gate.sh` and its package wiring. No reusable Dazzle Nix module or installer is added.
- Regression tests: remote pane IDs and keys reject shell metacharacters; session, workspace, agent flags, path output, and model output keep their wire shapes; clipboard paste runs probe, upload, and copy only through `sh`; the removed gate route has no tests or compatibility branch left.
- Ownership: `lib/platform/`, `lib/application/`, `lib/features/hosts/`, `lib/features/shell/`, README, tests, and the user's two host configuration repositories. One commit per repository when the user asks to commit.

### 39.49 The screen offers a VNC password when the server has no `None`, so a Mac's Screen Sharing answers (user, 2026-09-05: "la integración de vnc con mac no funciona y quizá no soporte ssh tunneling (sí lo hace)"; "implement it then")
- The diagnosis first, since the guess was the tunnel: macOS Remote Login forwards `direct-tcpip` by default, so the channel to `127.0.0.1:5900` opens. What fails is the next line. Apple Screen Sharing offers security types 30, 33, 35 and 36, plus 2 once "VNC viewers may control screen with password" is ticked, and never `None`; the client spoke `None` only, so every Mac ended on `auth unsupported`. Amends 39.32's protocol and reason lines and 39.48's README line; "VNC password auth" leaves 39.32's not-built list.
